\displaystyle T_{p,1},\to\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\xi_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(\xi_{i}^{2}-1)

in distribution as
p\to\infty.
The proof is completed.
\square

Now we begin to prove Proposition
1
. As far as the proofs go, parts (i) and (ii) from the lemma have different natures, to make the presentation clear, we will handle the two parts separately.

Proof of Proposition
1
(i)
. Assume
\boldsymbol{\mu}=0
. Then
\mathbb{X}_{1},\cdots,\mathbb{X}_{n}
are i.i.d. with distribution
N_{p}(\mathbb{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
, where all of the
p^{2}
entries of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
are identical to
\sigma^{2}
. For this reason, we write

{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}=\xi_{i}(1,\cdots,1)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
for
i=1,\cdots,n
, where
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{n}
are i.i.d.
N(0,\sigma^{2})
. By (
1
) and (
7
),
\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}
,

\displaystyle\hat{{\bf S}}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}})({\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}})^{T}~~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~~\hat{{\bf R}}=\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}\hat{{\bf S}}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2},

where
\hat{{\bf D}}
is the diagonal matrix of
\hat{{\bf S}}
. Set
\bar{\xi}=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\xi_{i}
and
W=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(\xi_{i}-\bar{\xi})^{2}
. Then
\sqrt{n}\bar{\xi}/\sigma\sim N(0,1)
,
nW/\sigma^{2}\sim\chi^{2}(n-1)
, and
\bar{\xi}
and
W
are independent. Since
\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=\bar{\xi}(1,\cdots,1)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
, we see
{\boldsymbol{X}}_{i}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=(\xi_{i}-\bar{\xi})^{T}(1,\cdots,1)^{T}\in\mathbb{R}^{p}
. Hence,
\hat{{\bf S}}=W\cdot\mathbb{J}
, where
\mathbb{J}
is a
p\times p
matrix whose entries are all equal to
1
. It follows
\hat{{\bf D}}=W\cdot\mathbb{I}_{p}
and
\hat{{\bf R}}=\mathbb{J}
. In particular,
\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})=p^{2}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=\frac{np}{n-1}\cdot\frac{(\sqrt{n}\bar{\xi}/\sigma)^{2}}{nW/[(n-1)\sigma^{2}]}\overset{d}{=}\frac{np}{n-1}\cdot F_{1,n-1}.

(200)

By (
2
),

\displaystyle T_{SD}=\frac{n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}-p(n-1)(n-3)^{-1}}{\sqrt{2[\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})-p^{2}(n-1)^{-1}]}}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n}{n-1}\cdot F_{1,n-1}-\frac{n-1}{n-3}}{\sqrt{2[1-(n-1)^{-1}]}}.

(201)

Notice
F_{1,n-1}\to\chi^{2}(1)
as
n\to\infty
. We see from the Slutsky lemma that
T_{SD}\to\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot[\chi^{2}(1)-1]
as
p\to\infty
. Finally, by (
9
) and (
200
),

\displaystyle T_{p,1}=\frac{n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}-pn(n-3)^{-1}}{\sqrt{2\big|\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})-p(p-1)(n-1)^{-1}\big|}}\overset{d}{=}\frac{\frac{np}{n-1}\cdot F_{1,n-1}-pn(n-3)^{-1}}{\sqrt{2\big|p^{2}-p(p-1)(n-1)^{-1}\big|}}.

As a consequence,

\displaystyle T_{p,1}

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n}{n-1}\cdot F_{1,n-1}-n(n-3)^{-1}}{\sqrt{2\{1-(p-1)/[(n-1)p]\}}}\to\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot\big[\chi^{2}(1)-1\big].

The proof is completed.
\square

Proof of Proposition
1
(ii)
. Recall

\displaystyle T_{SD}=\frac{n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}-p(n-1)(n-3)^{-1}}{\sqrt{2[\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})-p^{2}(n-1)^{-1}]}}.

Since
T_{SD}
is scale-invariant, without loss of generality, we assume
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}=\mathbb{I}_{p}
. By (
194
),

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}\sim N_{p}(\mathbb{0},\mathbb{I}_{p}),\ n\hat{{\bf S}}\sim W_{p}(m,\mathbb{I}_{p}),\ \mbox{and}\ \bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}\ \mbox{and}\ \hat{{\bf S}}\ \mbox{are independent},

where
m:=n-1
and
W_{p}(m,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
is the Wishart distribution defined after (
165
). Also, by (
195
),
\hat{{\bf D}}
and the diagonal matrix of
\frac{1}{n}W_{p}(m,\mathbb{I}_{p})
have the same distribution. By definition of
W_{p}(m,\mathbb{I}_{p})
, its
p
diagonal entries are i.i.d.
\chi^{2}(m)
. Therefore,
n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}
is a sum of
p
i.i.d. random variables with distribution
n\cdot\frac{N(0,1)^{2}}{\chi^{2}(m)}
, where the numerator and denominator are independent. Thus, we are able to write

\displaystyle n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=\frac{n}{m}\sum_{i=1}^{p}X_{p,i},

(202)

where
X_{p,1},\cdots,X_{p,p}
are i.i.d. with distribution
F(1,m)
. By Lemma
6.36
,

\displaystyle U_{p}:=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2p}}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{p}X_{p,i}-\frac{mp}{m-2}\Big)\to N(0,1)

(203)

as
p\to\infty
, regardless of the speeds of
n
and
p
going to infinity. Solve
\sum_{i=1}^{p}X_{p,i}
in terms of
U_{p}
to see
\sum_{i=1}^{p}X_{p,i}=\sqrt{2p}U_{p}+\frac{mp}{m-2}
. By plugging this into (
202
), we obtain

\displaystyle n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}=\frac{n}{m}\sqrt{2p}U_{p}+\frac{np}{m-2}.

(204)

Consequently,

\displaystyle n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}-p(n-1)(n-3)^{-1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{n}{m}\sqrt{2p}U_{p}+\frac{np}{m-2}-\frac{mp}{m-2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{2p}U_{p}+\frac{\sqrt{2p}}{m}U_{p}+\frac{p}{m-2}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2p}}\big[n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}-p(n-1)(n-3)^{-1}\big]=U_{p}+\frac{1}{m}U_{p}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{2}(m-2)}.

(205)

On the other hand,
\hat{{\bf R}}=\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}\hat{{\bf S}}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}
by (
7
). Write
n\hat{{\bf S}}=(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\xi}_{p})^{T}(\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\xi}_{p})
, where
\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1},\cdots,\boldsymbol{\xi}_{p}
are i.i.d.
N_{m}(0,\mathbb{I}_{m})
. Set

\mathbb{e}_{i}=\frac{\boldsymbol{\xi}_{i}}{\|\boldsymbol{\xi}_{1}\|}
for
i=1,\cdots,p
. Then
\{\mathbb{e}_{1},\cdots,\mathbb{e}_{p}\}
are i.i.d. uniformly distributed on the
m
-dimensional sphere; see, for example, p. 38 from
Muirhead 1982
. Also,
\hat{{\bf R}}=(\mathbb{e}_{i}^{T}\mathbb{e}_{j})
. Set

V_{p}:=\frac{1}{p}\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq p}\big[m(\mathbb{e}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbb{e}_{j})^{2}-1\big].

We claim

\displaystyle\sup_{p\geq 2}\mbox{Var}(V_{p})\leq\frac{3}{2}.

(206)

In fact, by Lemma 11 from
Cai et al. 2013
or (22) in Lemma 4.1 from
Cai and Jiang 2012
,
\{\mathbb{e}_{i}^{T}\mathbb{e}_{j};\,1\leq i<j\leq p\}
are pairwise i.i.d. Thus,
\mbox{Var}(V_{p})=\frac{1}{p^{2}}\cdot\frac{1}{2}p(p-1)\cdot m^{2}\cdot\mbox{Var}((\mathbb{e}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbb{e}_{2})^{2}).
Since
\mathbb{e}_{1}
and
\mathbb{e}_{2}
are independent, by Theorems 1.5.6 and 1.5.7 from
Muirhead 1982
, we know
\mathbb{e}_{1}^{T}\mathbb{e}_{2}\overset{d}{=}\xi_{1}(\xi_{1}^{2}+\cdots+\xi_{m}^{2})^{-1/2}
, where
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{m}
are i.i.d.
N(0,1)
. In particular, if
n=2
then
m=1
,
\mathbb{e}_{1}^{T}\mathbb{e}_{2}
is a symmetric Bernoulli random variable, and hence
(\mathbb{e}_{1}^{T}\mathbb{e}_{2})^{2}=1
and
\mbox{Var}((\mathbb{e}_{1}^{T}\mathbb{e}_{2}))=0
. Now, for
n\geq 3
,

\displaystyle\mbox{Var}((\mathbb{e}_{1}^{\prime}\mathbb{e}_{2})^{2})=\mbox{Var}\Big(\frac{\xi_{1}^{2}}{\xi_{1}^{2}+\cdots+\xi_{m}^{2}}\Big)\leq E\frac{\xi_{1}^{4}}{(\xi_{1}^{2}+\cdots+\xi_{m}^{2})^{2}}=\frac{3}{m(m+2)}

by taking
a_{1}=2
and other
a_{i}=0
in Lemma 2.4 from Jiang (2012). The inequality is true for all
m\geq 1
. Combining this and the earlier expression of
\mbox{Var}(V_{p})
, we obtain (
206
). In particular, by the Chebyshev inequality, (
206
) indicates

\displaystyle\frac{V_{p}}{m}\to 0

(207)

as
p\to\infty
regardless of the speeds of
n
and
p
going to infinity. Write

\displaystyle\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq p}(\mathbb{e}_{i}^{\prime}\mathbb{e}_{j})^{2}=\frac{p}{m}V_{p}+\frac{p(p-1)}{2m}.

Since
\hat{{\bf R}}
is a symmetric matrix whose diagonal entries are all equal to
1
. The above implies

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})=p+\frac{2p}{m}V_{p}+\frac{p(p-1)}{m}.

(208)

Consequently, we have from (
207
) that

\displaystyle\frac{2}{p}\cdot\Big[\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})-\frac{p^{2}}{m}\Big]=2+\frac{4}{m}V_{p}-\frac{2}{m}\to 2

(209)

in probability. It follows from this and (
205
) that

\displaystyle T_{SD}=\frac{\sqrt{2p}}{\sqrt{p(2+4m^{-1}V_{p}-2m^{-1})}}\cdot\Big[U_{p}+\frac{1}{m}U_{p}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{2}(m-2)}\Big].

By (
207
) and the Slutsky lemma,
\frac{\sqrt{2p}}{\sqrt{p(2+4m^{-1}V_{p}-2m^{-1})}}\to 1
in probability. Therefore, by (
203
),

\displaystyle U_{p}+\frac{1}{m}U_{p}+\frac{\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{2}(m-2)}\to\begin{cases}\xi_{0},&\text{if $p/n^{2}\to 0$};\\
\xi_{0}+\sqrt{h/2},&\text{if $p/n^{2}\to h$};\\
\infty,&\text{if $p/n^{2}\to\infty$}\end{cases}

in distribution, where
\xi_{0}\sim N(0,1)
. So we get part (ii) of Proposition
1
.

Finally, as for
T_{p,1},
in lieu of (
9
),

\displaystyle T_{p,1}=\frac{n\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}-pn(n-3)^{-1}}{\sqrt{2\big|\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})-p(p-1)(n-1)^{-1}\big|}}=\frac{\frac{n}{m}\sqrt{2p}U_{p}}{\sqrt{2p+\frac{4p}{m}V_{p}}}=\frac{\frac{n}{m}U_{p}}{\sqrt{1+\frac{2}{m}V_{p}}}

by (
204
) and (
208
). This implies
T_{p,1}\to N(0,1)

as
p\to\infty
by (
203
) and (
207
), regardless of the speeds of
n
and
p
going to infinity.

\square

Proof of Theorem
2
.
Recall

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{i}=\frac{1}{n_{i}}\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}\mathbb{X}_{ij}\ \mbox{for}\ i=1,2;

\displaystyle\hat{{\bf S}}=\frac{1}{n_{1}+n_{2}}\Big[\sum_{j=1}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{X}_{1j}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{1})(\mathbb{X}_{1j}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{1})^{T}+\sum_{j=1}^{n_{2}}(\mathbb{X}_{2j}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{2})(\mathbb{X}_{2j}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{2})^{T}\Big].

The
p\times p
matrix
\hat{{\bf D}}
is the diagonal matrix of
\hat{{\bf S}}
and
\hat{{\bf R}}=\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}\hat{{\bf S}}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}
is the
p\times p
pooled sample correlation matrix. In particular, all of the diagonal entries of
\hat{{\bf R}}
are
1
. Thus,
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}\geq\sqrt{p}
. Now,
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{i}
is independent of
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}(\mathbb{X}_{ij}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{i})(\mathbb{X}_{ij}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{i})^{T}
for each
i=1,2
; see, for example, Theorem 3.1.2 from
Muirhead 1982
. Second, by assumption, the two samples are independent. Therefore, the three random vectors
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
,
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{2}
and
\hat{{\bf S}}
are independent. In particular,
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{1}
and
\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{2}
are independent of
\hat{{\bf D}}
. By assumption
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{1}={\boldsymbol{\mu}}_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\sim N_{p}\Big(\mathbb{0},\Big(\frac{1}{n_{1}}+\frac{1}{n_{2}}\Big){\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\Big).

Hence,

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)\sim N_{p}(\mathbb{0},{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}).

(210)

Furthermore, for each
i
,
\sum_{j=1}^{n_{i}}(\mathbb{X}_{ij}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{i})(\mathbb{X}_{ij}-\bar{\mathbb{X}}_{i})^{T}\sim W_{p}(n_{i}-1,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
; see, for example, Theorem 3.1.2 from
Muirhead 1982
. Therefore,
(n_{1}+n_{2})\hat{{\bf S}}\sim W_{p}(n_{1}+n_{2}-2,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
by independence. Consequently,

\displaystyle\hat{{\bf D}}_{1}:=\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}\hat{{\bf D}}\ \overset{d}{=}\,\mbox{the diagonal matrix of}\ \frac{1}{m}W_{p}\big(m-1,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}\big),

(211)

where
m:=n_{1}+n_{2}-1.
As explained earlier, the left hand side of (
210
) is independent of
\hat{{\bf D}}_{1}
. In particular, by replacing “
\boldsymbol{\eta}
” and “
n
” from (
174
) with “
\sqrt{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)
” and “
m
”, respectively, we obtain

\displaystyle E\Big[\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})\Big]

(212)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle E\Big[\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}_{1}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)p}{n_{1}+n_{2}-4}.

This is the reason how come up with the numerator of
T_{p,2}
defined in (
16
).
The assumption
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{p}{(n_{1}+n_{2})^{a}}=0
implies
n_{1}+n_{2}\to\infty
as
p\to\infty.
Thus we know from assumption (b) that
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{p}{m\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=0
.
Replacing “
n
” with “
m
” in Lemma
4.1
, we have from (
210
) and (
211
) that

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}_{1}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})-p}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf R}^{2})}}

(213)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}{\bf D}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})-p}{\sqrt{2\,\mbox{tr}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}}+o_{p}(1),

where
{\bf D}
is the diagonal matrix of
{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}
,
{\bf R}={\bf D}^{-1/2}{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}}{\bf D}^{-1/2}
is the population correlation matrix. By (
196
) and (
210
),

\displaystyle\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}{\bf D}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})\overset{d}{=}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\lambda_{i}\xi_{i}^{2},

(214)

where
\lambda_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{p}\geq 0
are the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{R}
and
\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{p}
are i.i.d.
N(0,1).
Similar to the argument as in
Step 2
in the proof of Theorem
1
, we see

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}\Big(-p+\sum_{i=1}^{p}\lambda_{i}\xi_{i}^{2}\Big)\to\sqrt{2}\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\xi_{0}+\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(\xi_{i}^{2}-1)

in distribution, where
\xi_{0}\sim N(0,1)
and
\xi_{0}
is independent of
\xi_{1},\xi_{2},\cdots
. Combining this with (
213
) and (
214
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})-p}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf R}^{2})}}

(215)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}_{1}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})-p}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf R}^{2})}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\xi_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(\xi_{i}^{2}-1)

in distribution.
Evidently,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf R}^{2})}}\Big[p-\frac{(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)p}{n_{1}+n_{2}-4}\Big]=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\frac{-3p}{(n_{1}+n_{2}-4)\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}\to 0

by the assumption
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{p}{(n_{1}+n_{2})\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=0
. Add the left hand sides of the above two assertions to obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}\big(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2}\big)^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})-\frac{(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)p}{n_{1}+n_{2}-4}}{\sqrt{2\mathrm{tr}({\bf R}^{2})}}

(216)

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\xi_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(\xi_{i}^{2}-1)

in distribution. Next we will replace
\mathrm{tr}({\bf R}^{2})
by its ratio-unbiased-estimator.

Recall
(n_{1}+n_{2})\hat{{\bf S}}\sim W_{p}(n_{1}+n_{2}-2,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
, and
\hat{{\bf D}}
is the diagonal matrix of
\hat{{\bf S}}
and
\hat{{\bf R}}=\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}\hat{{\bf S}}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1/2}
. Set
\kappa=\frac{n_{1}+n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}
. Then

\displaystyle\kappa\hat{{\bf S}}\sim\frac{1}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}W_{p}(n_{1}+n_{2}-2,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}});

\displaystyle\hat{{\bf R}}=(\kappa\hat{{\bf D}})^{-1/2}(\kappa\hat{{\bf S}})(\kappa\hat{{\bf D}})^{-1/2}\ \ \mbox{and}\ \ \kappa\hat{{\bf D}}\ \mbox{is the diagonal matrix of}\ \kappa\hat{{\bf S}}.

The essential assumption from Theorem
3
is that, in its own notation,
\hat{\mathbb{R}}
is the sample correlation matrix obtained from
\hat{{\bf S}}\sim\frac{1}{n}W_{p}(n-1,{\boldsymbol{\Sigma}})
. Replace “
\hat{{\bf S}}
” and “
n
” from Theorem
3
with “
\kappa\hat{{\bf S}}
” and “
n_{1}+n_{2}-1
”, respectively. Assumption (b) indicates
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{p}{(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=0
and
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{p}{(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)^{a}}=0
. Then by Theorem
3
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\mbox{tr}(\mathbb{R}^{2})}\Big[\mbox{tr}(\hat{\mathbb{R}}^{2})-\frac{p(p-1)}{n_{1}+n_{2}-2}\Big]\to 1

(217)

in probability.
This together with (
216
) and the Slutsky lemma yields

\displaystyle T_{p,2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\frac{n_{1}n_{2}}{n_{1}+n_{2}-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})^{T}\hat{{\bf D}}^{-1}(\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{1}-\bar{{\boldsymbol{X}}}_{2})-\frac{(n_{1}+n_{2}-1)p}{n_{1}+n_{2}-4}}{\sqrt{2\big|\mathrm{tr}(\hat{{\bf R}}^{2})-\frac{p(p-1)}{n_{1}+n_{2}-2}\big|}}

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle\Big(1-\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}\Big)^{1/2}\xi_{0}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}(\xi_{i}^{2}-1)

in distribution as
p\to\infty
.

Finally, by the same argument as deriving (
193
), we see
T_{SD}^{\prime}=T_{p,2}\cdot[1+o_{p}(1)]+o_{p}(1).
So the conclusion for
T_{SD}^{\prime}
follows from the above and the Slutsky lemma. The proof is completed.
\square

6.4
A Lemma and Verification of
(
11
), (
12
) and (
13
)

The following lemma is used in the discussion after Theorem
2
.

L
EMMA
6.37

Let
\{\mathbb{R}_{p};\,p\geq 1\}
be non-negative definite matrices whose diagonal entries are all equal to
1
. Let
\lambda_{p,1}\geq\cdots\geq\lambda_{p,p}\geq 0
be the eigenvalues of
\mathbb{R}_{p}
. Assume condition “
C4
” stated in Theorem 2 from
Zhang et al. 2020b
hold, that is,
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{p,i}}{\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}}=\rho_{i}
for all
i\geq 1
with
\rho_{1}>0
and
\lim_{p\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\lambda_{p,i}}{\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}<\infty
. Then
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}=1
.

Proof of Lemma
6.37
. Notice
\sum_{i=1}^{p}\lambda_{p,i}=\mbox{tr}(\mathbb{R}_{p})=p
because all of the diagonal entries of
\mathbb{R}_{p}
are identical to
1
. The assumption
\lim_{p\to\infty}\sum_{i=1}^{p}\frac{\lambda_{p,i}}{\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}<\infty
implies that

\displaystyle\frac{p}{\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}}\to\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}

(218)

as
p\to\infty
. Set
a_{p,i}=\frac{\lambda_{p,i}}{\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}}.
Then
\lim_{p\to\infty}a_{p,i}=\rho_{i}
and
a_{p,1}^{2}+\cdots+a_{p,p}^{2}=1
by the definition of
\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}
. For any
K\geq 1
, write

\displaystyle a_{p,1}^{2}+\cdots+a_{p,K}^{2}=1-\sum_{i=K+1}^{p}a_{p,i}^{2}.

(219)

We claim that
\lim_{K\to\infty}\limsup_{p\to\infty}\sum_{i=K+1}^{p}a_{p,i}^{2}=0
. If this is true, by letting
p\to\infty
first and then sending
K\to\infty
, then
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}=1
. We now prove the claim. In fact, write

\displaystyle\sum_{i=K+1}^{p}a_{p,i}=\frac{p}{\|\mathbb{R}_{p}\|_{F}}-\sum_{i=1}^{K}a_{p,i}.

For fixed
K\geq 1
, let
p\to\infty
and use (
218
) to have

\displaystyle\lim_{p\to\infty}\sum_{i=K+1}^{p}a_{p,i}=\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}\Big)-\sum_{i=1}^{K}\rho_{i}=\sum_{i=K+1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}.

Use the assumption
\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}<\infty
and let
K\to\infty
to see
\lim_{K\to\infty}\limsup_{p\to\infty}\sum_{i=K+1}^{p}a_{p,i}=0
. The claim is then verified since
a_{p,i}\leq 1
for all
1\leq i\leq p
.
\square
.

In the following the notation “
A_{p}\sim B_{p}
” means that
A_{p}/B_{p}\to 1
as
p\to\infty
.

The Verification of (
11
)
. Review Example
2.1
. We have

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}=\begin{pmatrix}1&r&r&\cdots&r\\
r&1&r&\cdots&r\\
\vdots&&&&\\
r&r&r&\cdots&1\end{pmatrix}_{p\times p}.

Easily,
\lambda_{1}=1+(p-1)r
and
\lambda_{2}=\cdots=\lambda_{p}=1-r
. To maintain
\mathbb{R}
to be non-negative definite, all eigenvalues have to be non-negative, that is,
-\frac{1}{p-1}\leq r\leq 1.
Recall
r=r_{p}
satisfies that
\lim_{p\to\infty}\sqrt{p}\cdot r=c
. Now we consider three cases:
c=0
,
c\in(0,\infty)
and
c=\infty
.

Case 1:
c=0
. In this case
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}^{2}=[1+(p-1)r]^{2}+(p-1)(1-r)^{2}\sim p
as
p\to\infty
, thus
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}\sim\sqrt{p}
. Easily,
\frac{p}{n\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=O(\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n})\to 0
provided
p=o(n^{2})
. Also,
\rho_{1}=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=0
, hence
\lambda_{i}=0
for every
i\geq 1
.
By Theorem
1
, both
T_{SD}
and
T_{p,1}
converge to
N(0,1)
in distribution.

Case 2:
c\in(0,\infty)
. In this case
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}^{2}=[1+(p-1)r]^{2}+(p-1)(1-r)^{2}\sim(c^{2}+1)p
as
p\to\infty
, hence
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}\sim\sqrt{c^{2}+1}\cdot\sqrt{p}
. Readily,

\displaystyle\frac{p}{n\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=O\Big(\frac{\sqrt{p}}{n}\Big)\to 0~~~~\mbox{and}~~~~\rho_{1}=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}\to\frac{c}{\sqrt{c^{2}+1}};

\displaystyle\rho_{i}=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=0~~\mbox{for every}~~i\geq 2

provided
p=o(n^{2})
. By Theorem
1
, under condition
p=o(n^{2})
, we know both
T_{SD}
and
T_{p,1}
converge to

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\sqrt{c^{2}+1}}\xi_{0}+\frac{c}{\sqrt{2(c^{2}+1)}}(\xi_{1}^{2}-1)

in distribution, where
\xi_{0}
and
\xi_{1}
are i.i.d.
N(0,1).

Case 3:
c=\infty
. In this case
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}^{2}=[1+(p-1)r]^{2}+(p-1)(1-r)^{2}\sim(pr)^{2}
as
p\to\infty
. Therefore,
\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}\sim pr
. Notice
\lim_{p\to\infty}p/(n\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F})=\lim_{p\to\infty}1/(nr_{p})=0
,
\rho_{1}=\lim_{p\to\infty}\lambda_{1}/\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}=1
and
\rho_{i}=0
for
i\geq 2
provided
nr_{p}\to\infty
.
So, under conditions
nr_{p}\to\infty
and
p=o(n^{a})
for some constant
a>0
, we have
T_{p,1}\to\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}[\chi^{2}(1)-1]
.
\square

The Verification of (
12
)
. Recall
r\in(0,1)
,
m=[p^{r}]
and
\mathbb{A}_{m}
from Example
2.1
. By definition,

\displaystyle\mathbb{R}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbb{A}_{m}&\mathbb{0}\\
\mathbb{0}&\mathbb{I}_{p-m}\end{pmatrix}.

Then the largest eigenvalues of
\mathbb{R}
is
\lambda_{1}=1+(m-1)r
and the rest of them are
\lambda_{2}=\cdots=\lambda_{m}=1-r
and
\lambda_{m+1}=\cdots=\lambda_{p}=1
. Then

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}^{2}=[1+(m-1)r]^{2}+(m-1)(1-r)^{2}+(p-m)=m^{2}r^{2}+p+O(m).

Thus,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}=\begin{cases}\sqrt{p}\cdot[1+o(1)],&\text{if $0<r<\frac{1}{2}$};\\
\sqrt{5p/4}\cdot[1+o(1)],&\text{if $r=\frac{1}{2}$};\\
rp^{r}\cdot[1+o(1)],&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<r<1$.}\end{cases}

Also,

\displaystyle\rho_{1}=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{1}}{\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=\begin{cases}0,&\text{if $0<r<\frac{1}{2}$};\\
1/\sqrt{5},&\text{if $r=\frac{1}{2}$};\\
1,&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<r<1$.}\end{cases}

Obviously,
\rho_{i}=0
for
i\geq 2.

At last,

\displaystyle\frac{p}{n\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=\begin{cases}O(\sqrt{p}/n),&\text{if $0<r\leq\frac{1}{2}$};\\
O(p^{1-r}/n),&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<r<1$.}\end{cases}

Assume
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\boldsymbol{0}
. Then, we have from Theorem
1
that both
T_{SD}
and
T_{p,1}
go to

\displaystyle\begin{cases}N(0,1),&\text{if $0<r<\frac{1}{2}$};\\
\frac{2}{\sqrt{5}}N(0,1)+\frac{1}{\sqrt{10}}\cdot[\chi^{2}(1)-1],&\text{if $r=\frac{1}{2}$};\\
\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\cdot[\chi^{2}(1)-1],&\text{if $\frac{1}{2}<r<1$}\end{cases}

in distribution under condition
p=o(n^{2})
for
0<r\leq\frac{1}{2}
and
p=o(n^{1/(1-r)})
for
\frac{1}{2}<r<1
, where the random variables
N(0,1)
and
\chi^{2}(1)
are independent as
r=\frac{1}{2}
.
\square

The Verification of (
13
)
. Recall
m=[\log(p+2)].
Given
\tau\geq 0
, we have
p^{\prime}=p-[\tau\sqrt{p}]
,
\lambda_{i}=1+\tau 2^{-i}\big(1-2^{-m}\big)^{-1}\sqrt{p}
for
1\leq i\leq m
and
\lambda_{i}=1
for
i=m+1,\cdots,p^{\prime}-1
,
\lambda_{p^{\prime}}=1+[\tau\sqrt{p}]-\tau\sqrt{p}\in[0,1]
and the rest of
\lambda_{i}
are zero. Obviously,
\lambda_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{k}\geq k
for each
i=1,\cdots,p^{\prime}-1
and
\lambda_{1}+\cdots+\lambda_{p^{\prime}}
is identical to

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big[1+\tau 2^{-i}\big(1-2^{-m}\big)^{-1}\sqrt{p}\big]+(p^{\prime}-m-1)\cdot 1+\big(1+[\tau\sqrt{p}]-\tau\sqrt{p}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(m+\tau\sqrt{p})+\big(p-[\tau\sqrt{p}]-m-1\big)+1+[\tau\sqrt{p}]-\tau\sqrt{p}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle p,

where we use the fact
\sum_{i=1}^{m}2^{-i}\big(1-2^{-m}\big)^{-1}=1
.
Lemma
4.3
tells us that there exists a correlation matrix
\mathbb{R}
such that
\mathbb{R}
has eigenvalues
\lambda_{i},\,1\leq i\leq p.
Now,

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{m}\big[1+\tau 2^{-i}\big(1-2^{-m}\big)^{-1}\sqrt{p}\big]^{2}+(p^{\prime}-m-1)\cdot 1^{2}+\big(1+[\tau\sqrt{p}]-\tau\sqrt{p}\big)^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tau^{2}p\big(1-2^{-m}\big)^{-2}\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{4^{i}}+p+O\big(\sqrt{p}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{\tau^{2}}{3}+1\Big)p+o(p)

since
\sum_{i=1}^{m}\frac{1}{4^{i}}\to\frac{1}{3}
as
p\to\infty
. In particular,
\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{p}{n\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=0
provided
p=o(n^{2})
. Easily, for each
i\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\rho_{i}:=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{\lambda_{i}}{\|\mathbb{R}\|_{F}}=\lim_{p\to\infty}\frac{1+\tau 2^{-i}\big(1-2^{-m}\big)^{-1}\sqrt{p}}{\sqrt{(\frac{\tau^{2}}{3}+1)p+o(p)}}\to\sqrt{\frac{3\tau^{2}}{\tau^{2}+3}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{i}}

as
p\to\infty
. Now,

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\rho_{i}^{2}=\frac{3\tau^{2}}{\tau^{2}+3}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{4^{i}}=\frac{\tau^{2}}{\tau^{2}+3}.

Assuming
{\boldsymbol{\mu}}=\boldsymbol{0}
, by Theorem
1
, we see that

T_{p,1}\to\sqrt{\frac{3}{\tau^{2}+3}}\xi_{0}+\sqrt{\frac{3\tau^{2}}{2(\tau^{2}+3)}}\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\frac{1}{2^{i}}(\xi_{i}^{2}-1)
in distribution, where
\xi_{0},\xi_{1},\cdots,\xi_{d}
are i.i.d.
N(0,1).
